) of Theorem
4.1
, to guarantee that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Gx^{K}+v^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we need to impose
\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}^{2}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{2}}}{(K+s)^{2}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}^{2}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)\eta}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}]}}
.
This requirement leads to the choice of
k
as
K:=\big\lfloor\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}}{\epsilon}\big\rfloor
.

Let us estimate the expected total number
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]
of oracle calls
\mathbf{G}_{\xi}y^{k}
for (
VFOG
), which is
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&2\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{4n^{1/3}}{c_{1}}-s}\big(c_{1}n^{2/3}+\frac{4n}{k+s+1}\big)+4c_{1}n^{2/3}\big(K-\frac{4n^{1/3}}{c_{1}}+s\big)\\
&\leq&4n+4n\ln\big(\frac{4n^{1/3}}{c_{1}}+1\big)+\frac{4c_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}n^{2/3}}{\epsilon}\\
&=&\mathcal{O}\left(n\ln(n^{1/3})+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}n^{2/3}}{\epsilon}\right).\end{array}

This implies that
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]\leq\mathcal{O}\big(n\ln(n^{1/3})+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}n^{2/3}}{\epsilon}\big)
as stated in (
40
).

\square

Finally, we estimate the complexity of (
VFOG
) using the
L-SARAH
estimator.

Corollary 4

Suppose that Assumptions
2
-
4
hold for (
GE
) under the finite-sum setting (
2
).
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k},z^{k})\}
be generated by (
VFOG
) using the SARAH estimator
\widetilde{G}y^{k}
from (
L-SARAH
) for
Gy^{k}
and the parameters as in Theorem
4.1
.
For given
\nu\in[0,1]
and
c_{1}\in(0,n^{1-\nu}]
, if
n\geq\big[\frac{2\Gamma\eta}{c_{1}\rho_{c}}\big]^{1/\nu}
for
\Gamma
given in (
31
), and
b
and
\mathbf{p}_{k}
in (
L-SARAH
) are chosen as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}b_{k}=b:=\lfloor c_{1}n^{\nu}\rfloor\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\frac{\Gamma\eta}{c_{1}\rho_{c}n^{\nu}}+\frac{2}{k+s+1}&\textrm{if}~k\leq\lfloor\frac{2c_{1}\rho_{c}n^{\nu}}{\Gamma\eta}-s\rfloor,\\
\frac{2\Gamma\eta}{c_{1}\rho_{c}n^{\nu}}&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{array}

(42)

Then, we have
1\leq b_{k}\leq n
and
\mathbf{p}_{k}\in(0,1]
.
For any given
\epsilon>0
, the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbf{G}_{\xi}y^{k}
is at most

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]&=&\mathcal{O}\left(n\ln(n^{\nu})+n^{2\nu}+\frac{\mathcal{R}_{0}(n^{1-\nu}+n^{\nu})}{\epsilon}\right)\end{array}

(43)

to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Gx^{K}+v^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
If we choose
\nu=\frac{1}{2}
, then
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=\mathcal{O}\big(n\ln(n^{1/2})+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\epsilon}\big)
.

Proof

By Lemma
3
,
\widetilde{G}y^{k}
generated by (
L-SARAH
) satisfies Definition
2
with
\kappa_{k}=\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
\Theta_{k}=\frac{1}{b_{k}}
.
Using these values, the condition (
31
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{p}_{k}\geq\frac{\Gamma\eta}{\rho_{c}b_{k}}+\frac{2}{k+s+1}.\end{array}

Let us choose
b:=\lfloor c_{1}n^{\nu}\rfloor
as in (
42
) for some
\nu\in[0,1]
and
c_{1}>0
.
Then, the last condition holds if we choose
\mathbf{p}_{k}
as in (
42
).
Moreover, to ensure
0<\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
, we need to impose
b_{k}=c_{1}n^{\nu}\geq\frac{2\Gamma\eta}{\rho_{c}}\geq 1
, leading
to

n\geq\big[\frac{2\Gamma\eta}{c_{1}\rho_{c}}\big]^{1/\nu}
as stated.
In addition, we also have
b_{k}\leq n
.

Now, from (
32
) of Theorem
4.1
, to guarantee that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Gx^{K}+v^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we need to impose
\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}^{2}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{2}}}{(K+s)^{2}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}^{2}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)\eta}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}]}}
.
This requirement leads to the choice of
K:=\big\lfloor\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}}{\epsilon}\big\rfloor
.
In this case, the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbf{G}_{\xi}y^{k}
is
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where
n
is the evaluations of the first epoch
k=0
, and
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}
is the evaluations of the next
K
iterations.
Then, we can evaluate
\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&:=&\sum_{k=1}^{K}[n\mathbf{p}_{k}+2(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})b]\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}(n\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b)\\
&\leq&\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{2c_{1}n^{\nu}}{\Gamma}-s}\big(\frac{\Gamma n^{1-\nu}}{c_{1}}+\frac{2n}{k+s+1}+2c_{1}n^{\nu}\big)+\big(K-\frac{2c_{1}n^{\nu}}{\Gamma}\big)\big(\frac{2\Gamma n^{1-\nu}}{c_{1}}+2c_{1}n^{\nu}\big)\\
&\leq&2n+2n\ln\big(\frac{2c_{1}n^{\nu}}{\Gamma}+2\big)+\frac{4c_{1}^{2}n^{2\nu}}{\Gamma}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{C}_{0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}}{\epsilon}\big(\frac{2n^{1-\nu}}{\Gamma}+2c_{1}n^{\nu}\big)\\
&=&\mathcal{O}\left(n\ln(n^{\nu})+n^{2\nu}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{0}}(n^{1-\nu}+n^{\nu})}{\epsilon}\right).\end{array}

This expression together with
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
lead to (
43
).
Finally, if we choose
\nu=\frac{1}{2}
, then
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=\mathcal{O}\big(n\ln(n^{1/2})+\frac{n^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\big)
.

\square

Remark 2

Note that both
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
b_{k}
selected in Corollaries
2
,
3
, and
4
aim at achieving the best oracle complexity.
Other choices of these parameters still work as long as they satisfy the condition
\kappa_{k}\geq\frac{\Gamma\eta}{\rho_{c}}\Theta_{k}+\frac{2}{k+s+1}
in Theorem
4.1
.
For instance, the probability
\mathbf{p}_{k}
in Corollary
4
can be fixed at
\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\frac{\Gamma\eta}{c_{1}\rho_{c}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{2}{s+1}
for all
k\geq 1
and
s>2
.
This choice is more convenient for the implementation of (
VFOG
) in practice.

Remark 3

Compare to our recent work
TranDinh2024
, Assumption
4
is more restrictive than the weak-Minty condition in
TranDinh2024
.
However, (
VFOG
) achieves a better convergence rate by a factor of
\frac{1}{k}
and a better oracle complexity by a factor
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
.
Moreover, (
VFOG
) also works with biased estimators.
For SARAH, it improves by a factor
n^{1/6}
over SVRG and SAGA.
Compared to
alacaoglu2021stochastic
;
alacaoglu2021forward
;
cai2022stochastic
, (
VFOG
) can work with a class of nonmontone operators, and its convergence guarantee is in
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Gx^{K}+v^{K}\|^{2}\big]
in contrast to a gap function as in
alacaoglu2021stochastic
;
alacaoglu2021forward
, which only work for the monotone case.
Therefore, it is inconsistent to compare their oracle complexity.
Nevertheless, since th methods in
alacaoglu2021stochastic
;
alacaoglu2021forward
are non-accelerated, we believe that their convergence rate is at most
\mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big)
, leading to a complexity with
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
factor worst than ours.
Other works such as
davis2022variance
;
tran2024accelerated
;
TranDinh2025a
require stronger assumptions such as [star] co-coercivity and strong quasi-monotonicity, excluding our problems of interest in this paper.

Remark 4

We only consider the finite-sum case (
2
) in Corollaries
2
,
3
, and
4
.
If we consider the general case
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\mathbf{G}_{\xi}(\cdot)\big]=G(\cdot)
, then we can also prove that the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]
is at most
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-3})
to achieve an
\epsilon
-solution.
However, we skip this analysis.

5
The Detailed Proofs of Theorems
3.1
,
3.2
, and
4.1

This section provides the detailed proofs of three main theorems: Theorems
3.1
,
3.2
, and
4.1
.

5.1
The full proof of Theorem
3.1

The proof of Theorem
3.1
is divided into the following steps.

5.1.1
Key technical lemmas for convergence analysis

For given
x^{k}
,
y^{k-1}
,
y^{k}
, and
v^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, we introduce the following quantities of our analysis:

\begin{array}[]{lclclcl}\hat{w}^{k}&:=&Gy^{k-1}+v^{k}&\textrm{and}&\quad w^{k}&:=&Gx^{k}+v^{k},\\
e^{k}&:=&\widetilde{G}y^{k}-Gy^{k}&\textrm{and}&\quad\hat{e}^{k}&:=&\widetilde{G}y^{k-1}-Gx^{k}.\end{array}

(44)

Our first step is to establish the following key lemma, whose proof is in Appendix
B.1
.

Lemma 4

Assume that Assumptions
2
,
3
, and
4
hold for (
GE
).
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k},z^{k})\}
be updated by (
VFOG
) using the following parameters:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k+1}:=t_{k}+1,\quad\gamma_{k}:=\frac{\gamma(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}},\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{k}:=\frac{(c_{1}\eta+c_{2}\eta+2\rho_{n})(t_{k}-s)-\gamma_{k}}{t_{k}},\end{array}

(45)

for some constants
\gamma\geq 0
,
c_{1}>0
, and
c_{2}>0
, determined later.
Let
w^{k}
,
\hat{w}^{k}
,
e^{k}
, and
\hat{e}^{k}
be defined by (
44
),
v^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
be defined by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}:=\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+st_{k-1}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{2\gamma_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(46)

Then, for any constant
\omega\geq 0
, we have

\hskip-17.22217pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\eta t_{k}^{2}}{2}\big[(1-\alpha)\big(|\!\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}|\!\|^{2}-\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big)+\omega L^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\rho_{c}t_{k}(t_{k}-s)|\!\|Gx^{k+1}-Gx^{k}|\!\|^{2}+\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s-M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k})}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta st_{k}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{a_{k}-\hat{a}_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+s(s-1)\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&-{~}b_{k}\|e^{k}\|^{2}-\hat{b}_{k}\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

(47)

where
M^{2}:=2(1+\omega)L^{2}
,
\psi:=(1-c_{1}-c_{2})\eta-2\rho_{n}>0
, and

\hskip 0.0pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}a_{k+1}&:=&\psi t_{k}^{2}+2s\rho_{n}t_{k}-\gamma(t_{k}-1),\\
\hat{a}_{k}&:=&\psi(t_{k}-s)^{2}+s(\eta+2\rho_{n})(t_{k}-s)+\frac{2\gamma{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(t_{k}-1)^{2}}}{t_{k}},\\
b_{k}&:=&\big(\frac{1}{2c_{1}}+M^{2}\eta^{2}\big)\eta t_{k}^{2},\\
\hat{b}_{k}&:=&\big(\frac{\beta_{k}^{2}t_{k}}{2c_{2}\eta}+\frac{\gamma(t_{k}-1)}{2t_{k}}+M^{2}\eta^{3}t_{k}\big)t_{k}+\frac{\gamma(s-1)(t_{k}-1)}{2}\big(\frac{2}{t_{k}}+t_{k}-2\big).\end{array}\right.\hskip 0.0pt

(48)

Next, we can further simplify Lemma
4
by choosing concrete values for the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
to achieve a simplified bound for
\mathcal{P}_{k}
as stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 5

Under the same conditions and parameters as in Lemma
4
, suppose that

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}s\geq 2,\qquad t_{k}\geq s+1,\qquad\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\leq 1,\qquad\gamma<16\eta,\quad\textrm{and}\quad(3s-2)\eta\geq 8(s-1)\rho_{n}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(49)

Then,
\beta_{k}
in (
45
) satisfies
\beta_{k}\leq\big[\frac{(s-2)\eta}{4(s-1)}+2\rho_{n}]\frac{(t_{k}-s)}{t_{k}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
defined by (
46
) satisfies

\hskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\eta t_{k}^{2}}{2}\big[(1-\alpha)\big(|\!\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}|\!\|^{2}-\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big)+\omega L^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\rho_{c}t_{k}(t_{k}-s)|\!\|Gx^{k+1}-Gx^{k}|\!\|^{2}+\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\big(1-\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\big)\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta st_{k}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\phi_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+s(s-1)\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&-{~}\varphi t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{(5s-6)\eta t_{k}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k}\|^{2}-\varphi t_{k-1}^{2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(50)

where

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\phi_{k}&:=&\big[\frac{(s-2)}{2}\eta-4(s-1)\rho_{n}-3\gamma\big](t_{k}-1)+\frac{(s-1)(s-2)\eta}{4}+2(2s^{2}-3s+1)\rho_{n}+\gamma,\\
\varphi&:=&\frac{2(5s-6)\eta}{s-2}+(s-1)\gamma.\end{array}\right.

(51)

Remark 5

Lemma
5
only provides one choice for the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
.
This choice affects the ranges of parameters
\eta
,
\beta_{k}
, and
\gamma_{k}
in (
VFOG
) as well as the range of
L\rho_{n}
.
In this paper, we do not try to optimize the choice of these constants.
We only provide one instance to simplify our convergence analysis.
However, other possible choices still work.

Finally, for
\mathcal{P}_{k}
defined by (
46
), we can lower bound it as in the following lemma.

Lemma 6

Under the same settings as in Lemma
5
,
\mathcal{P}_{k}
defined by (
46
) is lower bounded by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{10\gamma}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2},\end{array}

(52)

where
A_{k}:=\big[\frac{(3s-2)\eta-5\gamma}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}\big]t_{k-1}^{2}+s(2\rho_{n}-\rho_{*})t_{k-1}-\gamma(t_{k-1}-1)
.

5.1.2
The Lyapunov function and additional supporting lemmas

The Lyapunov function.
We consider the following Lyapunov function:

\hskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\mathcal{P}_{k}+\frac{\omega\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}\eta(1-\kappa)(s+1)^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{(11s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}12})\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-4.30554pt

(53)

where
\Lambda_{0}
is given in (
18
),
\kappa
is in (
15
), and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
is the function defined by (
46
).

Next, we need the following supporting lemmas to simplify the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
The proof of these lemmas can be found in Appendix
C
.

Lemma 7

Under the same settings as in Theorem
3.1
,
\mathcal{L}_{k}
given in (
53
) satisfies

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\epsilon_{1}\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\eta st_{k}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(2s+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta(1-\kappa)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}-\Lambda_{0}\eta\delta_{k},\end{array}

(54)

where
\epsilon_{1}:=1-(s+1)M^{2}\eta^{2}\geq 0
and
\Lambda_{0}
is given in (
18
).

Lemma 8

Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k},z^{k})\}
be generated by (
VFOG
) and the conditions of Lemma
5
hold.
Suppose further that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\right]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\!\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}|\!\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(55)

Then, we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0,\ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{1}{t_{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty.

(56)

In addition, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|y^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(57)

Lemma 9

Under the same settings as in Lemma
8
, for
\mathcal{P}_{k}
given in (
46
), if
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k}\big]\leq\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
for all
k\geq 0
and a given
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{0}\geq 0
, then
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\big]
exists for any
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.

If, additionally,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}
exists,
\{t_{k-1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\}
is bounded, and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty
, then

\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0.

(58)

5.1.3
The proof of Theorem
3.1

For the sake of clarity, let us divide this proof into the following steps.

Step 1
(
The summability results in (
20
)
).

First,
applying
Lemma
7
, it follows from (
54
) after taking the full expectation
that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\eta s(k+s+1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\right]+\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{(2s+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\kappa)}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}{s^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k-1}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{\epsilon_{1}\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]-\Lambda_{0}\eta\delta_{k}.\end{array}

(59)

Summing up (
59
) from
k=0
to
k:=K
and noting that
\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq 0
due to Lemma
6
and denoting
\mathcal{S}_{K}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta_{k}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\eta s(k+s+1)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\right]&\leq&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\mathcal{L}_{0}+2\Lambda_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{K}},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)\rho_{n}\big](k+1)\mathbb{E}\left[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\right]&\leq&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}\mathcal{L}_{0}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}\Lambda_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{K}},\\
\end{array}

(60)

Since
z^{0}=x^{0}=y^{-1}
and the conventions
e^{-1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta_{-1}}=0
, we have from (
53
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{0}&=&\mathcal{P}_{0}=\frac{a_{0}}{2}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{2\gamma_{0}}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{[(3s-2)(s-2)s^{2}-4(s-1)^{2}]\eta}{8(s-1)}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s(s^{2}-1)(s-2)}{2\eta}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Here, we have used
a_{0}=[\frac{(3s-2)\eta}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}]s^{2}+2s^{2}\rho_{n}-\frac{(s-1)\eta}{s-2}=\frac{(3s-2)s^{2}\eta}{4(s-1)}-\frac{(s-1)\eta}{s-2}
and
\gamma_{0}=\frac{\eta(t_{0}-1)}{(s-2)t_{0}}=\frac{\eta s}{(s-2)(s+1)}
.
Substituting this
\mathcal{L}_{0}
into (
60
), we obtain (
20
).

Step 2
(
The proof of the bounds in (
22
)
).

Now, from (
59
), by induction, we also have
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k}\big]\leq\mathcal{L}_{0}+\Lambda_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{k}
.
However, from (
52
) and (
53
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq\mathcal{P}_{k}&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{10\gamma}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\geq\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

(61)

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}A_{k}&:=&\big[\frac{(3s-2)\eta-5\gamma}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}\big]t_{k-1}^{2}+s(2\rho_{n}-\rho_{*})t_{k-1}-\gamma(t_{k-1}-1)\\
&\geq&\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-2)(s-1)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}>0.\end{array}

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)(\mathcal{L}_{0}+\Lambda_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{k})}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+s)^{2}}=\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)\eta(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}+\Lambda_{0}\mathcal{S}_{k})}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+s)^{2}},\end{array}

which is the first bound of (
22
), where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=\frac{[(3s-2)s^{2}+4(s-1)^{2}]}{8(s-1)}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s(s^{2}-1)(s-2)}{2\eta^{2}}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

From (
53
), since

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\omega\eta t_{k-1}^{2}&=&\big[\frac{11s-13}{2(s-2)}+\frac{\Theta(s+1)^{2}(18s-23)}{2(s-2)[s^{2}-(1-\kappa)(s+1)^{2}]}+1\big]\eta t_{k-1}^{2}\\
&\geq&A_{k}=\big[\frac{(3s-2)\eta-5\gamma}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}\big]t_{k-1}^{2}+s(2\rho_{n}-\rho_{*})t_{k-1}-\gamma(t_{k-1}-1),\end{array}

by
the
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
G
and
Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|Gx^{k}-Gy^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{4}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{1}{4}\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-1)(s-2)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Combining this inequality, (
52
), and (
53
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\geq&\mathcal{P}_{k}+\frac{\omega\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-1)(s-2)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Hence, utilizing this inequality, with similar arguments as the proof of the first line of (
22
), we obtain the second line of (
22
) from this expression.

Step 3
(
Other intermediate summability results
).

Next, since
\epsilon_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=1-(s+1)M^{2}\eta^{2}}>0
(due to the choice
\eta<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\lambda}{L}}
as in (
19
)) and
\mathcal{S}_{\infty}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\delta_{k}<+\infty
, we can rearrange (
59
) and then use Lemma
11
to prove that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k}\big]
exists and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]}&<&+\infty,\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}(t_{k}-s)\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(62)

By Assumption
2
and Young’s inequality, we easily get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}&\leq&L^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2},\\
\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+2\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2},\\
\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+2\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Moreover, we also have
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]+2\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]
.
Using these relations, we can obviously derive from (
62
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(63)

Step 4
(
Applying Lemmas
8
and
9
).

First, from
(
62
)
and (
63
), we see that the conditions (
55
) of Lemma
8
are fulfilled.
Next, from (
59
), by induction, we get
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k}\big]\leq\mathcal{L}_{0}+\eta\Lambda_{0}\mathcal{S}_{\infty}
for all
k\geq 0
.
Moreover, by (
61
), we have
\mathcal{P}_{k}\leq\mathcal{L}_{k}
.
Therefore, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k}\big]\leq\mathcal{L}_{0}+\eta\Lambda_{0}\mathcal{S}_{\infty}\equiv\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
.

Next
, by (
53
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\mathcal{P}_{k}+\frac{\omega\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}\eta(1-\kappa)(s+1)^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{(11s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}12})\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since t
he last three terms are summable due to (
62
), their limit is zero.

Using these facts and the existence of
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k}\big]
proved above,
we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k}\big]
also exists.

From the first line of (
22
), we also say that
\{t_{k-1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\}
is bounded, and from the second line of (
20
), we have
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty
.
Overall, we have verified all the conditions of Lemma
9
.
Hence, we obtain (
23
) from (
58
).

Step 5
(
Iteration-complexity
).

For a given tolerance
\epsilon>0
, from (
22
) and
\mathcal{S}_{k}\leq\mathcal{S}_{\infty}<+\infty
, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Gx^{k}+v^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we need to impose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{C_{0}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}+\Lambda_{0}S_{\infty})}{(k+s)^{2}}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
This relation implies that
k=\mathcal{O}\big(1/\epsilon\big)
, which proves our statement (d).

\square

5.2
The proof of Theorem
3.2
: Almost sure convergence

Similar to the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we also divide this proof into several steps as follows.

Step 1
(
Almost sure summability bounds)
.

For
\mathcal{L}_{k}
given in (
53
), from (
54
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\epsilon_{1}\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\eta st_{k}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(2s+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\kappa)}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}-\Lambda_{0}\eta\delta_{k}.\end{array}

(64)

Let us define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}U_{k}&:=&\mathcal{L}_{k}+\frac{\epsilon_{1}\eta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}-w^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta st_{k-1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(65)

Then, we can rearrange (
64
) and
use

U_{k}
from (
65
) to get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[U_{k+1}\big]&\leq&U_{k}-\frac{\epsilon_{1}\eta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}-w^{k-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta st_{k-1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}8}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-}\frac{(2s+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\kappa)}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}+\Lambda_{0}\eta\delta_{k}.\end{array}

(66)

Note that
\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq 0
almost surely for all
k\geq 0
and
\mathcal{S}_{\infty}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\delta_{k}<+\infty
by our assumption, applying Lemma
13
to the last inequality, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}U_{k}
exists almost surely.
Moreover, we also have the following almost sure summability bounds:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+s)\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)\|w^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+s+1)^{2}\|e^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)(k+s+1)\Delta_{k}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)(k+s+1)\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+s)^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(67)

The first two lines of (
67
) are exactly (
24
).
Similar to the proof of (
63
), but using (
67
), we can also show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely}.\end{array}

(68)

From (
67
) we also have
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-s)\|\hat{w}^{k}-w^{k-1}\|^{2}=0
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely.
Combining these facts and the existence of
\lim_{k\to\infty}U_{k}
almost surely, (
65
) implies that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}_{k}
also exists almost surely.

Step 2
(
The bounds and almost sure convergence of
v^{k}:=s(x^{k}-z^{k})
).

Let
v^{k}:=s(x^{k}-z^{k})
.
Then, similar to the proof of (
92
), we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|v^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\|v^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{s}{t_{k}}\|v^{k}\|^{2}+4s\eta^{2}t_{k}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{4(2s+1)^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{s}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}+4s\eta^{2}t_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{4(s+1)^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{s}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(69)

By (
15
) and
1-\kappa\leq 1
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&\leq&\Delta_{k-1}+\Theta L^{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{k}}{t_{k}^{2}}.\end{array}

(70)

Combining (
69
) and (
70
), and noting that
\kappa\in(0,1]
, we also get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|v^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\|v^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{s}{t_{k}}\|v^{k}\|^{2}+4s\eta^{2}t_{k}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{4(s+1)^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{s}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{4(2s+1)^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{s}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}+4s\eta^{2}t_{k}\Delta_{k-1}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}4}s\eta^{2}L^{2}t_{k}\Theta\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}4}s\eta^{2}\delta_{k}}{t_{k}}.\end{array}

Note that the
third
to the
seventh
terms of this expression are almost surely summable due to (
67
) and (
68
).
Moreover, we also have
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\delta_{k}}{t_{k}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\delta_{k}<+\infty
.
Applying again Lemma
13
to the last inequality and using these facts, we conclude that almost surely

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}\ \ \textrm{exists \quad and \quad}\ \sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}}{t_{k}}<+\infty.\end{array}

(71)

Moreover, by Lemma
12
we also obtain from (
71
) that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely.

Step 3
(
The proof of (
25
)
).

First, from (
93
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\big(1+\frac{\eta}{st_{k}^{2}}\big)\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\big(\frac{\eta t_{k}}{s}+\frac{\eta\rho_{*}}{s}+\frac{2\eta^{2}}{s^{2}}\big)\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta}{st_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}+\big(\frac{\eta t_{k}^{2}}{s}+\frac{2\eta^{2}}{s^{2}}\big)\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(72)

Since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\eta}{st_{k}^{2}}<+\infty
and the last three terms of (
72
) are almost surely summable due to
t_{k}=k+s
, (
67
), and (
71
),
applying Lemma
13
to (
72
), we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
exists almost surely.

Next, from (
53
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\mathcal{P}_{k}+\frac{\omega\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}\eta(1-\kappa)(s+1)^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s^{2}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{(11s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}12})\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

However, the last three terms are almost surely summable due to (
67
), their limit is zero.
Hence, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}
also exists almost surely.

Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}
exists almost surely, and
\mathcal{P}_{k}\geq\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}
with
a_{k}=\mathcal{O}\left(t_{k-1}^{2}\right)
, there exists
\bar{M}>0
such that
t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\leq\bar{M}^{2}
almost surely.
Using this inequality and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|=0
almost surely, we can show that almost surely

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k-1}|\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle|&\leq&t_{k-1}\|w^{k}\|\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|\leq\bar{M}\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|\to 0\quad\text{as}~k\to\infty.\end{array}

This expression implies that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle=0
almost surely.

Now, recall
\mathcal{P}_{k}
from (
46
) as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}=\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+st_{k-1}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{2\gamma_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
exist almost surely and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle=0
almost surely, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}
exists almost surely.
However, since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k-1}\|w^{k}\|^{2}<+\infty
due to (
67
), applying Lemma
12
, we can easily argue that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely.
This limit implies the first limit of (
24
).
Note that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k-1}^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+2t_{k-1}^{2}\|Gx^{k}-Gy^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|Gx^{k}-Gy^{k-1}\|^{2}=0
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely, the last inequality implies that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}=0
, which leads to the second limit of (
24
).

Step 4
(
The almost sure convergence of iterates
).

Note that
\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|\leq\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|+\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|
.
Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|=0
almost surely and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|
exists almost surely, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|
exists almost surely.
From the second line of (
67
), we have
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|Gx^{k}+v^{k}\|=0
almost surely, for
v^{k}\in Tx^{k}
.
This means that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|w^{k}\|=0
almost surely for
(x^{k},w^{k})\in\mathrm{gra}(\Phi)
.
Moreover,
\mathrm{gra}(\Phi)
is closed by our assumption.
Applying Lemma
14
from the appendix, we conclude that
\{x^{k}\}
converges almost surely to a
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
-valued random variable
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.
Finally, since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|=0
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-y^{k}\|=0
, we can also conclude that both
\{z^{k}\}
and
\{y^{k}\}
also converges almost surely to the same
x^{\star}
.

\square

5.3
The full proof of Theorem
4.1

Before presenting the full proof of Theorem
4.1
, we need the following supporting lemma, whose proof is given in Appendix
C.4
.

Lemma 10

Let
\hat{\omega}
,
\hat{\lambda}
, and
\hat{\mu}
be defined by (
29
).
For (
GE
), suppose that
G
satisfies Assumptions
2
and
4
with
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
\rho_{c}>0
such that
L\rho_{n}\leq\hat{\mu}
.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k},z^{k})\}
be generated by (
VFOG
) using a variance-reduced estimator
\widetilde{G}y^{k}
for
Gy^{k}
satisfying Definition
2
.
Suppose that
s>2
and
\eta
,
\beta_{k}
, and
\gamma_{k}
are respectively updated by

\hskip-12.91663pt\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{8(s-1)\rho_{n}}{3s-2}\leq\eta\leq\frac{\hat{\lambda}}{L},\quad\gamma_{k}:=\frac{\eta(k+s)}{(s-2)(k+s+1)},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\beta_{k}:=\big[\frac{(s-2)\eta}{4(s-1)}+2\rho_{n}]\frac{k+1}{k+s+1}-\frac{\gamma_{k}}{k+s+1}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(73)

Suppose further that for a given small
\epsilon_{2}\geq 0
, there exists
\bar{c}
such that
\kappa_{k}
and
\Theta_{k}
in (
26
) of Definition
2
satisfy:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{3(16s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19})\eta\Theta_{k}t_{k}}{\rho_{c}(s-2)(t_{k}-s)}\leq\bar{c}\leq\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{2}}\Big[1-\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})t_{k}^{2}}{t_{k-1}^{2}}\Big].\end{array}

(74)

For
\bar{c}
in (
74
),
a_{k}
in Lemma
5
, and
\varphi:=\frac{(11s-13)\eta}{s-2}
, define the following
Lyapunov function
:

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&:=&\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+st_{k-1}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{2\gamma_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\rho_{c}t_{k-1}^{2}|\!\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}|\!\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(11s-12)\eta L^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t_{k-1}^{2}}}{s-2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-\bar{c})(16s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19})\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{(s-2)\bar{c}}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{(11s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}13})\eta t_{k-1}}{s-2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(75)

Then, the following inequality holds:

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\eta s(k+s+1)}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\phi_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+s(s-1)\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\big(1-\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\big)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{2\alpha\rho_{c}t_{k}^{2}}{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(16s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19})\eta\epsilon_{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\Delta_{k-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~-~\frac{(16s-19)\eta\delta_{k}}{\bar{c}(s-2)}},\end{array}\hskip 0.0pt

(76)

where
\phi_{k}:=\big[\frac{s^{2}-4s-2}{2(s-2)}\eta-4(s-1)\rho_{n}\big](k+s)+(s-1)\big[\frac{(s-2)\eta}{4}+2(2s-1)\rho_{n}\big]+\frac{\eta}{s-2}
.

5.3.1
The proof of Theorem
4.1

For the sake of clarity, let us divide this proof into the following steps.

Step 1
(
Verifying condition (
31
)
).

First, the update rules of parameters in (
30
) come from (
73
) of Lemma
10
.
Next, we verify the condition (
74
) of Lemma
10
.
Since we can choose
\epsilon_{2}
sufficiently small, (
74
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{3(16s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19})\eta\Theta_{k}t_{k}}{\rho_{c}(s-2)(t_{k}-s)}<1-\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})t_{k}^{2}}{t_{k-1}^{2}}.\end{array}

If we denote
\hat{C}:=\frac{3(16s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}19})}{s-2}
, then since
t_{k}=k+s+1
, this condition becomes

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{\hat{C}\Theta_{k}\eta(k+s+1)}{\rho_{c}(k+1)}<1-\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})(k+s+1)^{2}}{(k+s)^{2}}=\frac{\kappa_{k}(k+s+1)^{2}}{(k+s)^{2}}-\frac{2(k+s)+1}{(k+s)^{2}}.\end{array}

Because
k\geq 0
and
2(k+s)+1<2(k+s+1)
, this condition holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}\geq\frac{s^{2}\hat{C}\eta\Theta_{k}}{\rho_{c}(s+1)}+\frac{2}{k+s+1}=\frac{\Gamma\eta\Theta_{k}}{\rho_{c}}+\frac{2}{k+s+1},\end{array}

where
\Gamma:=\frac{3s^{2}(16s-18)}{(s-2)(s+1)}
is given in (
29
).
This condition is exactly (
31
).

Note that if there exist
\underline{\kappa}
and
\Theta>0
such that
0<\underline{\kappa}\leq\kappa_{k}<1
and
0<\Theta_{k}\leq\Theta
, then under the condition (
31
), we can easily show that there exists
\epsilon_{2}>0
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{\rho_{c}(s-2)(t_{k}-s)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}3(16s-19)}\eta\Theta_{k}t_{k}}\big[1-\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})t_{k}^{2}}{t_{k-1}^{2}}\big]-1\geq\epsilon_{2}>0.\end{array}

Step 2
(
The summability results in (
34
)
).

By (
5
) in Assumption
3
, we have
\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\geq-\rho_{*}\|w^{k}\|^{2}
.
Moreover, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\phi_{k}-2\rho_{*}s(s-1)&=&\big[\frac{s^{2}-4s-2}{2(s-2)}\eta-4(s-1)\rho_{n}\big](k+s)\\
&&+{~}(s-1)\big[\frac{(s-2)\eta}{4}+2(2s-1)\rho_{n}-2s\rho_{*}\big]+\frac{\eta}{s-2}\\
&\geq&\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{4(s-2)}>0.\end{array}

Substituting these facts into (
76
) in Lemma
10
, we get

\hskip-17.22217pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\eta s(k+s+1)}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{8(s-2)}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\big(1-\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\big)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2\alpha\rho_{c}t_{k}^{2}}{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{(16s-19)\eta\epsilon_{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\Delta_{k-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~-~\frac{(16s-19)\eta\delta_{k}}{\bar{c}(s-2)}}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(77)

Now, taking the full expectation
\mathbb{E}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of (
77
), we obtain

\hskip-12.91663pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k+1}\big]&\geq&\frac{\eta s(k+s+1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{8(s-2)}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{(16s-19)\eta\epsilon_{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k-1}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\big(1-\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{2\alpha\rho_{c}t_{k}^{2}}{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~-~\frac{(16s-19)\eta\delta_{k}}{\bar{c}(s-2)}}.\end{array}\hskip-25.83325pt

(78)

Summing up (
78
) from
k=0
to
k:=K
and noting that
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\geq 0
due to (
75
) and Lemma
6
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\eta s(k+s+1)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\right]&\leq&2(\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{K}}),\\
\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}]}{(s-2)}(k+1)\mathbb{E}\left[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\right]&\leq&8(\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{K}}),\end{array}

(79)

where
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
is defined in (
29
) and
\mathcal{S}_{K}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta_{k}
.

Since
z^{0}=x^{0}=y^{-1}
and the conventions
e^{-1}=0
, and
\Delta_{-1}=0
, we have from (
75
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}&=&\frac{a_{0}}{2}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{2\gamma_{0}}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}=\frac{[(3s-2)(s-2)s^{2}-4(s-1)^{2}]\eta}{8(s-1)}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s(s^{2}-1)(s-2)}{2\eta}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Here, we have used
a_{0}=[\frac{(3s-2)\eta}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}]s^{2}+2s^{2}\rho_{n}-\frac{(s-1)\eta}{s-2}=\frac{(3s-2)s^{2}\eta}{4(s-1)}-\frac{(s-1)\eta}{s-2}
and
\gamma_{0}=\frac{\eta(t_{0}-1)}{(s-2)t_{0}}=\frac{\eta s}{(s-2)(s+1)}
.
Substituting
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}
into (
79
), we obtain (
34
).

Step 3
(
The proof of the bounds in (
32
)
).

Now, from (
78
), by induction, we also have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\big]\leq\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}+\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{k}}
.
However, from
(
75
) and
(
52
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\mathcal{L}}}_{k}\geq\mathcal{P}_{k}&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{s^{2}(s-1)}{10\gamma}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\geq\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

(80)

where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}A_{k}&:=&\big[\frac{(3s-2)\eta-5\gamma}{4(s-1)}-2\rho_{n}\big]t_{k-1}^{2}+s(2\rho_{n}-\rho_{*})t_{k-1}-\gamma(t_{k-1}-1)\\
&\geq&\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-2)(s-1)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}>0.\end{array}

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)(\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\eta\mathcal{S}_{k}})}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+s)^{2}}=\frac{16(s-1)(s-2)\eta(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{k}})}{[(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+s)^{2}},\end{array}

which is the first bound of (
32
), where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=\frac{[(3s-2)(s-2)s^{2}-4(s-1)^{2}]}{8(s-1)}\|Gx^{0}+v^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{s(s^{2}-1)(s-2)}{2\eta^{2}}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

From (
75
), using

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\big[\frac{2(11s-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}12})\eta}{s-2}+2\rho_{c}\big]t_{k-1}^{2}\geq A_{k}=\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{4(s-1)(s-2)}-2\rho_{n}\big]t_{k-1}+s(2\rho_{n}-\rho_{*})t_{k-1}-\gamma(t_{k-1}-1),\end{array}

the
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
G
, the relation
|\!\|\cdot|\!\|\geq\|\cdot\|
, and Young’s inequality,
we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{(11s-12)\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\rho_{c}t_{k-1}^{2}|\!\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}|\!\|^{2}}\\
&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\big[\frac{(11s-12)\eta t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}+\rho_{c}t_{k-1}^{2}\big]\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{4}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{1}{4}\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-1)(s-2)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Combining this inequality, (
52
), and (
75
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&\geq&\mathcal{P}_{k}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{(11s-12)\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}+\rho_{c}t_{k-1}^{2}|\!\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}|\!\|^{2}}\\
&\geq&\frac{1}{4}\big[\frac{(3s^{2}-8s-1)\eta}{8(s-1)(s-2)}-\rho_{n}\big](k+s)^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Hence, utilizing this inequality, with similar arguments as the proof of the first line of (
32
), we obtain the second line of (
32
) from this expression.

Step 4
(
Other intermediate summability results
).

Next, rearranging (
78
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k+1}\big]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\big]-\frac{\eta s(k+s+1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]-\frac{[(s^{2}-4s-2)\eta-8(s-1)(s-2)\rho_{n}](k+1)}{8(s-2)}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta L^{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k}-y^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]-\frac{(16s-19)\eta\epsilon_{2}t_{k-1}^{2}}{s-2}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k-1}\big]-\frac{2\alpha\rho_{c}t_{k}^{2}}{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta t_{k}(t_{k}-s)}{2}\big(1-\frac{M^{2}\eta^{2}t_{k}}{t_{k}-s}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{(16s-19)\eta\delta_{k}}{\bar{c}(s-2)}.\end{array}

Since
\epsilon_{2}>0
and
\eta<{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\hat{\lambda}}{L}}
as in (
30
), the terms from the second to seventh on the right-hand side of this inequality are all nonnegative.
Under the assumption
\mathcal{S}_{\infty}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\delta_{k}<+\infty
, we can apply Lemma
11
to the last inequality and obtain that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\!\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}|\!\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}(t_{k}-s)\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(81)

Using the relations
|\!\|\cdot|\!\|\geq\|\cdot\|
and
\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]
from the first line of (
26
) after taking total expectations, we also obtain from (
81
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(82)

By Young’s inequality, we easily get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+2\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2},\\
\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+2\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Moreover, we also have
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]+2\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]
.
Using these relations, we can obviously derive from (
81
)
and (
82
)
that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\hat{e}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(83)

Step 5
(
Applying Lemmas
8
and
9
).

The proof of (
35
) follows the same arguments as the proof of
Step 4
in Theorem
3.1
and we omit.
The iteration-complexity stated in Statement (d) is also proven as in Theorem
3.1
, and we omit it here.

Step 6
(
Almost sure convergence
).

The proof of the almost sure convergence results in Statement (e) follow similarly the proof of Theorem
3.2
, and hence we omit.

\square

6
Numerical Experiments

In this section, we present two numerical examples to validate our algorithm, (
VFOG
), and compare it with the most related existing methods.
Since the majority of competitors requires monotonicity, we only choose convex-concave minimax problems.
Our code is written in Python, running on a MacBook Pro. Apple M4 with 24Gb Memory and 1Tb storage.

6.1
Bilinear matrix game: Policeman vs. Burglar problem

\mathrm{(a)}
Mathematical model.

Consider a city with
m^{2}
houses distributed on a grid
m\times m
, where the
j
-th house has wealth
w_{j}
for all
j=1,\cdots,m^{2}
.
Each night, a Burglar selects a house
j
to rob, while a Policeman chooses a location to station himself near house
k
.
Once the burglary begins, the Policeman is immediately informed of its location, and the probability that he apprehends the Burglar is given by
\mathbf{p}_{c}:=\exp\{-\theta\,\mathrm{dist}(j,k)\}
, where
\mathrm{dist}(j,k)
is the distance between houses
j
and
k
.
However, neither the Burglar nor the Policeman has access to the true wealth
w_{j}
; instead, they need to estimate it using a set of
n
observations
{\hat{w}^{(i)}_{j}}
for
i=1,\cdots,n
.
The Burglar aims to maximize his expected reward, which is
\mathcal{R}_{j}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\hat{w}^{(i)}_{j}}\left(1-\exp\{-\theta\,\mathrm{dist}(j,k)\}\right)
,
while Policeman’s objective is to minimize
\mathcal{R}_{j}
.

The Policeman vs. Burglar problem described above can be viewed as a zero-sum game.
It was studied in
nemirovski2013mini
and we slightly modify it to obtain a stochastic version.
Following
nemirovski2013mini
, we define a payoff matrix
\mathbf{L}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{L}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{L}^{(i)},\quad\mathbf{L}^{(i)}_{j,k}={\hat{w}^{(i)}_{j}}\left(1-\exp\{-\theta\,\mathrm{dist}(j,k)\}\right),\quad 1\leq j,k\leq m^{2}.\end{array}

Then, we can formulate the above problem into the following classical bilinear matrix game:

\min_{u\in\Delta_{p_{1}}}\max_{v\in\Delta_{p_{2}}}\Big\{\mathcal{L}(u,v):=\langle\mathbf{L}u,v\rangle\Big\},

(84)

where
u
and
v
represent mixed strategies of the Policeman and Burglar, and
\Delta_{p_{1}}
and
\Delta_{p_{2}}
are the standard simplexes in
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}
, respectively with
p_{1}=p_{2}=m^{2}
.

Let us
define
x:=[u;v]\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
p:=p_{1}+p_{2}
,
G_{i}x:=\mathbf{G}_{i}x=[\mathbf{L}^{(i)\top}v;-\mathbf{L}^{(i)}u]
for
i\in[n]
, and
Tx:=[\partial\delta_{\Delta_{p_{1}}}(u);\partial\delta_{\Delta_{p_{2}}}(v)]
, where
\delta_{\mathcal{X}}(\cdot)
is the indicator of
\mathcal{X}
.
Then, we can write the optimality condition of (
84
) as
0\in\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}x+Tx
, a special case of (
GE
).
Since
\mathbf{G}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{G}_{i}
is a skew-symmetric matrix,
G
is monotone and
L
-Lipschitz continuous.

\mathrm{(b)}
Input data.

Following
nemirovski2013mini
, we first choose
\mathrm{dist}(j,k):=|j-k|
and
\theta:=0.8
.
Next, we generate the nominal wealth
w_{j}
from a standard normal distribution, and then take
|w_{j}|
to ensure its nonnegativity.
Then, the noisy wealth
w^{(i)}_{j}
is generated as
w^{(i)}_{j}:=|w_{j}+\epsilon^{(i)}_{j}|
, where
\epsilon^{(i)}_{j}
is a normal random variable with zero mean and variance
\sigma^{2}=0.05
.
Finally, we produce two sets of experiments, each has
10
problem instances, as follows.

•

Experiment 1.

m=10
and
n=1000
, corresponding to
p_{1}=100
and
p=2p_{1}=200
.

•

Experiment 2.

m=15
and
n=2000
, corresponding to
p_{1}=225
and
p=2p_{1}=450
.

\mathrm{(c)}
Algorithms and parameters.

We choose
3
competitors as follows.

•

The deterministic optimistic gradient method, e.g., in
daskalakis2018training
, abbreviated by
OG
.
We consider this method as a baseline for our comparison.

•

The variance-reduced forward-reflected-backward splitting (FRBS) algorithm in
alacaoglu2021forward
, abbreviated by
VrFRBS
.

•

The variance-reduced extragradient (EG) algorithm in
alacaoglu2021stochastic
, abbreviated by
VrEG
.

Both
VrFRBS
and
VrEG
are variants of the EG method and use a loopless SVRG estimator.

We implement four variants of our method (
VFOG
):
VFOG-Sgd
,
VFOG-Svrg
,
VFOG-Saga
, and
VFOG-Sarah
, and compare them with
OG
,
VFRBS
, and
VEG
.
Here,
VFOG-Sgd
simply uses the mini-batch estimator (
16
), while the last three variants use L-SVRG, SAGA, and L-SARAH estimators, respectively.
We run all algorithms for 200 epochs, where each epoch corresponds to
n
oracle calls
\mathbf{G}_{\xi}
.

The parameters of these algorithms are chosen as follows.
For all 4 variants of our methods, we choose
\eta=\frac{1}{8L}
, (i.e.,
s=3
and
\omega=7
or
\hat{\omega}=7
).
For
OG
, we choose
\eta=\frac{1}{L}
.
For
VrFRBS
, we choose
\eta=\frac{0.95(1-\sqrt{1-\mathbf{p}})}{2L}
, and for
VrEG
, we choose
\eta=\frac{0.95\sqrt{1-\alpha}}{L}
with
\alpha:=1-\mathbf{p}
.

For the probability of reference points and mini-batch size, we choose:

•

\mathbf{p}=0.5n^{-1/3}
for
VFOG-Svrg
,
VrFRBS
, and
VrEG
.
For example, if
n=1000
, then
\mathbf{p}=0.05
, and if
n=2000
, then
\mathbf{p}=0.04
.

•

\mathbf{p}=0.5n^{-1/2}

for
VFOG-Sarah
.

Note that if
n=1000
, then
\mathbf{p}=0.016
, while if
n=2000
, then
\mathbf{p}=0.011
.

•

b_{k}=\lfloor 0.5n^{2/3}\rfloor
for
VFOG-Svrg
,
VFOG-Saga
,
VrFRBS
, and
VrEG
,
In this case, if
n=1000
, then
b_{k}=50
, and if
n=2000
, then
b_{k}=79
.

•

b_{k}=\lfloor 0.5n^{1/2}\rfloor
for
VFOG-Sarah
.
(e.g.,
b_{k}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}15}
for
n=1000
and
b_{k}=22
for
n=2000
).

•

For
VFOG-Sgd
, we choose the increasing mini-batch size
b_{k}=\max\{5,\min\{0.05(l+1)^{3},n\}\}
, where
l
is the epoch counter.
This choice guarantees
b_{k}
increasing, but not too fast as using
b_{k}=\mathcal{O}\big((k+s)^{3}\big)
.

The initial point is chosen as
x^{0}:=[\frac{1}{p_{1}}\cdot\texttt{ones($p_{1}$)};\frac{1}{p_{2}}\cdot\texttt{ones($p_{2}$)}]
for all methods.

\mathrm{(d)}
Numerical results.

The results of these experiments are presented in Figure
2
.

Figure 2:
The performance of
7
algorithms to solve the bilinear matrix game (
84
) on
2
experiments, averaging over
10
problem instances each.

From Figure
2
, we can see that with the theoretical parameters, two (
VFOG
) variants:
VFOG-Saga
and
VFOG-Sarah
significantly outperform the competitors in both experiments.
However,
VFOG-Saga
is slightly better than
VFOG-Sarah
since
VFOG-Saga
uses only one batch
\mathcal{B}_{k}
as opposed to two in
VFOG-Sarah
per iteration.
As a compensation,
VFOG-Saga
requires to store a table of
n
operator values.
Our
VFOG-Svrg
also works well, but it is worse than
VFOG-Saga
and
VFOG-Sarah
.
With its theoretical parameters,
VrFRBS
performs poorly as it is the worst.

VrEG
is still worse than the baseline scheme
OG
.
Our
VFOG-Sgd
performs well and better than other methods in early epochs, but then it is saturated at a certain level of accuracy, perhaps due to the effect of variance.

Next, we test the effect of the probability
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and the mini-bath size
b_{k}
of
\mathcal{B}_{k}
using the same experiments as in Figure
2
.
We decrease both
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
b_{k}
by half, but do not change
\mathcal{B}_{k}
in
VFOG-Sgd
.
Then, the results are revealed in Figure
3
.

Figure 3:
The performance of
7
algorithms with a smaller probability
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and mini-bath size
b_{k}
to solve the bilinear matrix game (
84
) on
2
experiments, averaging over
10
problem instances each.

In this case, the performance of control-variate methods
improved
, due to the increase of inner iterations in each epoch.
This improvement is observed across all variants and still consistent to the first test.

6.2
Markov decision processes

\mathrm{(a)}
Mathematical model.

Markov decision processes (MDPs) are commonly used to model sequential decision-making problems
puterman1994markov
, with applications in numerous areas such as reinforcement learning
mnih2015human
and healthcare
alagoz2010markov
;
grandclem2019solving
.

A finite MDP consists of a state space
\mathcal{S}:=\{1,2,\cdots,n\}
with
n
states and a finite action space
\mathcal{A}:=\{1,2,\cdots,m\}
with
m
actions.
Each state-action pair
(s,a)
is associated with a reward
r_{sa}
and a transition probability distribution
P_{sa}\in\Delta_{n}
over the next states.
Define
r_{\infty}:=\max_{s,a}r_{sa}
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
r_{sa}\geq 0
for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
.
Given a discount factor
\bar{\gamma}\in(0,1)
and an initial state distribution
\mathbf{p}_{0}\in\Delta_{n}
, a common objective in MDPs is to maximize the expected infinite-horizon discounted reward.

Following the same procedure as in
jin2020efficient
;
puterman1994markov
, this MDP problem can be reformulated into the following concave-concave saddle-point problem:

{\min_{v\geq 0,\|v\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}r_{\infty}}{1-\bar{\gamma}}}\max\limits_{\mu\in\Delta_{nm}}\Big\{\mathcal{L}(v,\mu):=(1-\bar{\gamma})\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\top}v+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{a=1}^{m}\mu_{sa}\left(r_{sa}+\bar{\gamma}P_{sa}^{\top}v-v_{s}\right)\Big\},}\vskip-2.15277pt

(85)

where
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
presents the expected discounted rewards starting from
each
state
s\in\mathcal{S}
, and
\mu_{sa}
(
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
) are the dual variables.

Next, define
x:=[v;\mu]\in\mathbb{R}^{n(m+1)}
as the concatenation of
v
and
\mu=[\mu_{1};\cdots;\mu_{n}]\in\mathbb{R}^{nm}
(the concatenation of
\mu_{s}
’s with
\mu_{s}=(\mu_{s1},\cdots,\mu_{sm})^{\top}
,
s=1,\cdots,n
).
For each
s\in\mathcal{S}
, let
B_{s}:=\left[\bar{\gamma}P_{s1}-e_{s},\cdots,\bar{\gamma}P_{sm}-e_{s}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
r_{s}:=(r_{s1},\cdots,r_{sm})^{\top}
,
\mathcal{B}^{+}_{n}(0,R):=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}:\|v\|_{2}\leq R\}
, and

{\begin{array}[]{lcl}G_{s}x:=\begin{bmatrix}n(1-\bar{\gamma})\mathbf{p}_{0,s}e_{s}+nB_{s}\mu_{s}\\
\mathbf{0}_{(s-1)m}\\
-nr_{s}-nB_{s}^{\top}v\\
\mathbf{0}_{(n-s)m}\end{bmatrix},\qquad Tx:=\begin{bmatrix}\partial\delta_{\mathcal{B}^{+}_{n}(0,\frac{\sqrt{m}r_{\infty}}{1-\bar{\gamma}})}(v)\\
\partial\delta_{\Delta_{nm}}(\mu)\end{bmatrix}.\end{array}}\vskip-2.15277pt

Then, the optimality condition of (
85
) can be written as (
GE
):
0\in Gx+Tx
, where
Gx:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s=1}^{n}G_{s}x
is a finite-sum representation over the state space
\mathcal{S}
.

\mathrm{(b)}
Experimental setup and input data.

We evaluate the performance of (
VFOG
) on solving problem (
85
) using randomly generated MDPs from the
garnet
testbed
archibald1995generation
;
bhatnagar2012garnet
, which is widely used for benchmarking algorithms in sequential decision-making.
Garnet MDPs are defined by a branching factor
n_{b}
, representing the proportion of reachable next states from each
(s,a)
pair.
Our setup is characterized by a tuple
(|\mathcal{S}|,|\mathcal{A}|,n_{b},\lambda)
, and then we consider two experiments:
Experiment 1
with
(|\mathcal{S}|,|\mathcal{A}|,n_{b},\lambda)=(2000,5,1000,0.9)
, leading to
p=12000
and
Experiment 2
with
(|\mathcal{S}|,|\mathcal{A}|,n_{b},\lambda)=(4000,10,2000,0.9)
, leading to
p=44000
.
For each setting, we generate 10 random MDP instances where rewards are sampled uniformly at random from
[0,1]
.

\mathrm{(c)}
Algorithms and parameters.

In this experiment, we use three variants of our (
VFOG
):
VFOG-Svrg
,
VFOG-Saga
, and
VFOG-Sarah
since they use control variate technique.
We again compare them with the baseline method
OG
, and two other competitors:
VrFRBS
and
VrEG
using SVRG estimators.
Unlike the previous example, we decide to select the parameters for all algorithms using a grid search procedure on the interval
\frac{1}{L_{B}}[10^{-5},10]
to search for the best stepsize
\eta
in all algorithms, where
L_{B}=\|B\|
.
Note that
L_{B}
is not the Lipschitz constant of
G
.
Applying this procedure, we obtain the following parameter
\eta
for each algorithm.

•

For our variants
VFOG-Svrg
,
VFOG-Saga
, and
VFOG-Sarah
, we find
\eta=\frac{1}{10^{3}L}
.

•

For the baseline method:
OG
, we get
\eta=\frac{1}{10^{2}L}
.

•

For
VrFRBS
, we obtain
\eta=\frac{0.95(1-\sqrt{1-\mathbf{p}})}{10^{2}2L}
.

•

For
VrEG
, we find
\eta=\frac{0.95\sqrt{1-\alpha}}{10^{3}L}
with
\alpha:=1-\mathbf{p}
.

As in the first example, we still choose
\mathbf{p}=0.5n^{-1/3}
and
b=\lfloor 0.5n^{2/3}\rfloor
for
VFOG-Svrg
,
VrFRBS
, and
VrEG
,
\mathbf{p}=0.5n^{-1/2}
and
b=\lfloor 0.5n^{1/2}\rfloor
for
VFOG-Sarah
.
The initial point is chosen as
x^{0}:=[\frac{1-\lambda}{r_{\infty}}\texttt{ones(n)};\frac{1}{n{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m}}\texttt{ones(n{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}m})}]
for all methods.

\mathrm{(d)}
Numerical results.

We run all the algorithms using the above setting on two experiments, and the results are revealed in Figure
4
.

Figure 4:
The performance of
7
algorithms to solve the infinite horizon discounted reward MDPs reformulation (
85
) on 2 experiments, averaging over 10 problem instances each.

As shown in Figure
4
, the three VFOG variants:
VFOG-Svrg
,
VFOG-Saga
, and
VFOG-Sarah
, consistently outperform both the baseline method (
OG
) and competing algorithms across the entire training horizon.
Among them,
VFOG-Svrg
achieves the lowest residual norm, while
VFOG-Sarah
demonstrates the strongest overall performance.
These results suggest that acceleration indeed provides a significant performance boost.

Finally, we examine the effect of the probability
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and the mini-batch size
b_{k}
on the performance of our methods by halving their values to obtain more inner iterations in each epoch.
The corresponding results are presented in Figure
5
.

Figure 5:
The performance of
7
algorithms to solve the infinite horizon discounted reward MDPs reformulation (
85
) on 2 experiments using smaller
\mathbf{p}
and batch size
b
, averaging over 10 problem instances each.

We observe that the three variants of (
VFOG
) outperform their non-accelerated counterparts.
A smaller mini-batch size
b_{k}
enables our algorithms to run more iterations, while a smaller probability
\mathbf{p}_{k}
reduces the number of full evaluations of
G
.
Consequently, both factors contribute to the speedup of the algorithms observed in Figure
5
.
